McKernel Rust Port Plan With Controlled Verification
Updated: 2026-05-26

Purpose
------------------------------------------------------------

This document records the Rust migration work completed so far and defines the
full granular plan for porting McKernel-owned implementation code to Rust. The
verification strategy is intentionally staged: each checkpoint tests one layer,
has a clear success result, and stops before later layers make debugging noisy.

Current QEMU boot-path status
------------------------------------------------------------

The active x86_64/Rocky validation path now has non-reboot
build/equivalence gates plus a first safe QEMU guest boundary. The 2026-05-24
setup installed Rocky QEMU/libvirt/image/debug tooling, exposed
`qemu-system-x86_64` through the Rocky `qemu-kvm` binary, confirmed the system
libvirt connection responds, confirmed Rust nightly plus LTP/XPMEM
prerequisites are ready, and added `scripts/qemu-mckernel-guest.sh`. Host
`/dev/kvm` is present when commands run outside the default sandbox, but the
default sandbox itself does not expose `/dev/kvm`; QEMU runs from inside the
sandbox should use `--accel auto` or `--accel tcg` unless the run is escalated
or otherwise placed in a context where KVM is exposed. A 2026-05-25 non-boot
KVM probe confirmed that unprivileged sandboxed QEMU still fails to initialize
KVM, while an escalated/root QEMU process can initialize KVM and then be
terminated by timeout without booting a guest.

The QEMU runner boots an existing Rocky/RHEL-family qcow2 through a disposable
overlay, cloud-init seed, SSH port-forward, serial log, optional read-only 9p
share, and guest-only command execution. It refuses host `/dev/*` image paths,
does not load host IHK modules, does not call host `mcreboot.sh`, and attempts
guest-side `mcstop+release.sh -k` cleanup when SSH came up. Static validation
passed `bash -n`, `--help`, dry-run command construction, invalid option
rejection, and non-reboot build/equivalence gates. A real system-QEMU McKernel
boot is still not proven until a Rocky 8.x guest image is supplied or generated
and the guest install/boot smoke is run inside that VM.

Latest QEMU check on 2026-05-26 confirmed `qemu-system-x86_64 --version`,
observed `/dev/kvm` in the current environment, validated
`scripts/qemu-mckernel-guest.sh --help`, validated
`scripts/qemu-mckernel-guest.sh --image
/tmp/mckernel-qemu-domigrate.ZtJIgE/base.qcow2 --accel auto --dry-run
--log-dir /tmp/mckernel-qemu-domigrate.ZtJIgE/log` command construction, and
ran a bounded TCG boot-process probe against that disposable blank qcow2. The
blank image timed out cleanly under the wrapper as expected. No real guest boot
ran.

The later `do_wait()` pass repeated the safe checks with
`/tmp/mckernel-qemu-dowait.tQCG5z/base.qcow2`: QEMU version, `/dev/kvm`, image
file-type checks, runner `--help`, dry-run command construction, and a bounded
TCG boot-process probe. Sandboxed QEMU could not bind the forwarded SSH port;
the escalated retry started QEMU and then timed out waiting for SSH from the
blank image, as expected. No real guest boot or host reboot path ran.

The wait-candidate pass repeated the safe checks with
`/tmp/mckernel-qemu-waitcand.yNsShP/base.qcow2`: QEMU version, `/dev/kvm`,
image file-type checks, runner `--help`, dry-run command construction, and a
bounded TCG boot-process probe. Sandboxed QEMU could not bind the forwarded SSH
port; the escalated retry started QEMU and then timed out waiting for SSH from
the blank image, as expected. No real guest boot or host reboot path ran.

The wait-zombie/reparent pass repeated the safe checks with
`/tmp/mckernel-qemu-waitzombie.vDIyU2/base.qcow2`: QEMU version, `/dev/kvm`,
image file-type checks, runner `--help`, dry-run command construction, and a
bounded TCG boot-process probe. The blank image timed out waiting for SSH as
expected. No real guest boot or host reboot path ran.

The wait-scan traversal pass repeated the safe checks with
`/tmp/mckernel-qemu-waitscan.ogpUBC/base.qcow2`: QEMU version, `/dev/kvm`,
image file-type checks, runner `--help`, dry-run command construction, and a
bounded TCG boot-process probe. The blank image timed out waiting for SSH as
expected, and no `qemu-system-x86_64` process remained. No real guest boot or
host reboot path ran.

The ptrace-detach body pass repeated the safe checks with
`/tmp/mckernel-qemu-ptracedetach.kFpjUx/base.qcow2`: `/dev/kvm`, Rust/C
fallback image file-type checks, runner `--help`, dry-run command

The signal/credential syscall wrapper pass repeated the safe checks with
`/tmp/mckernel-qemu-sigcred.GZSrDp/base.qcow2`: `/dev/kvm`, Rust/C fallback
image file-type checks, runner `--help`, dry-run command construction, and a
bounded TCG boot-process probe. The blank image timed out waiting for SSH as

The ten-slice syscall wrapper pass repeated the safe checks with

Update 2026-05-26 ten-slice syscall wrapper strict-core batch
------------------------------------------------------------

Latest batch moved ten syscall wrapper bodies through Rust while preserving
the C ABI wrapper, primitive callbacks, scalar no-SIMD Rust code generation,
and C fallback scaffolding:

- `times()` CPU-time accounting, child CPU-time aggregation, copyout, and
  current-time return shaping.
- `setpgid()` pid/pgid normalization, target process lookup, execed rejection,
  Linux-forward callback dispatch, and post-success pgid mutation.
- `setrlimit()`, `getrlimit()`, and `prlimit64()` argument routing into the
  existing prlimit implementation callback.
- `sysinfo()` zero/fill/copyout sequencing, `get_cpu_id()` CPU callback return
  shaping, `mlockall()` policy/log/result sequencing, `munlockall()` log/return
  sequencing, and `getcpu()` CPU/node copyout sequencing.
- Equivalence coverage for success paths, copyout failures, missing callback
  gates, process lookup failures, execed rejection, prlimit argument routing,
  mlockall policy branches, and getcpu partial-copy behavior.

The Syscall core strict ownership row moves 95% -> 96% in `migration.txt`.
Post-+50 strict-core movement is now about +239 points. The broad Syscall core
dashboard row is already capped at 100%, so this does not add broad dashboard
percentage. Standalone readiness remains about 40% because primitive user-copy,
Linux forwarding, process lookup/locking, time/CPU primitive callbacks,
syscall dispatch, default-path C fallback scaffolding, and broader high-risk
syscall bodies remain C-owned.

Previous update 2026-05-26 signal/credential syscall wrapper strict-core batch
------------------------------------------------------------

Previous batch moved signal and credential syscall wrapper-body sequencing
through Rust while preserving the C ABI wrapper, primitive callbacks, and C
fallback scaffolding:

- `kill()`, `tgkill()`, and `tkill()` target validation, siginfo construction,
  do-kill callback dispatch, return shaping, and missing-callback error
  shaping.
- `setresuid()`, `setreuid()`, `setuid()`, `setresgid()`, `setregid()`, and
  `setgid()` Linux-forward callback dispatch plus credential-refresh callback
  sequencing.
- `setfsuid()` and `setfsgid()` do-syscall callback dispatch,
  credential-refresh callback sequencing, and return shaping.
- Equivalence coverage for success, validation failure, negative forwarding
  returns, missing refresh callback, and uid/gid setfsid dispatch cases.

The Syscall core strict ownership row moves 94% -> 95% in `migration.txt`.
Post-+50 strict-core movement is now about +229 points. The broad Syscall core
dashboard row is already capped at 100%, so this does not add broad dashboard
percentage. Standalone readiness remains about 40% because Linux forwarding,
credential refresh source data, actual signal delivery, user-copy, locks,
allocation/free, syscall dispatch, default-path primitive bodies, and fallback
scaffolding remain C-owned.

Previous update 2026-05-26 ptrace syscall request-dispatch strict-core batch
------------------------------------------------------------

Previous batch moved top-level `ptrace()` request/callback body dispatch through
Rust while preserving the C ABI wrapper, primitive handler callbacks, and C
fallback scaffolding:

- `PTRACE_TRACEME`, kill/continue/singlestep/syscall wake requests, and
  register/fpreg/regset get/set request routing.
- User/text peek/poke request routing, including PEEKDATA/POKEDATA aliases.
- Set-options, attach/detach, siginfo/eventmsg, arch fallback, and
  unsupported/missing-callback error shaping.
- Equivalence coverage for every dispatch case, alias routing, null ops, and
  missing getregs/arch callbacks.

The Syscall core strict ownership row moved 93% -> 94% in `migration.txt`.
Post-+50 strict-core movement reached about +219 points. The broad Syscall core
dashboard row was already capped at 100%, so this did not add broad dashboard
percentage. Standalone readiness remains about 40% because thread lookup,
user-copy, register/fpreg/regset primitives, process-memory access, signal
delivery, allocation/free, default-path primitive bodies, and fallback
scaffolding remained C-owned.

Previous update 2026-05-26 ptrace-detach body strict-core batch
------------------------------------------------------------

Latest batch moved strict-core-only `ptrace_detach_thread()` main-body
sequencing through Rust while preserving the C ABI wrapper, primitive
callbacks, and C fallback scaffolding:

- Main-thread detach gating, zombie finalization selection, tracer child-list
  detach, and ptraced reparenting to the original parent.
- Report-list detach/reattach, ptrace cleanup/free callback dispatch,
  single-step clear dispatch, and exit-signal dispatch.
- Forwarded-signal siginfo construction, `do_kill()` callback sequencing,
  wake/release dispatch, and final process finalization dispatch.
- Equivalence coverage for a main-thread zombie detach with forwarded signal,
  a non-main no-report/no-forward detach, and invalid null-input rejection.

The Syscall core strict ownership row moves 92% -> 93% in `migration.txt`, and
the Process/VM strict ownership row moves 90% -> 91%. Post-+50 strict-core
movement is now about +209 points. The broad Syscall core and Process/VM
dashboard rows were already capped at 100%, so this does not add broad
dashboard percentage. Standalone readiness remains about 40% because primitive
lock/list/free/wakeup/signal callback bodies, syscall dispatch, user-copy
primitive bodies, ptrace lookup/orchestration, default-path C primitive bodies,
and fallback scaffolding remain C-owned.

Previous update 2026-05-26 wait-scan traversal strict-core batch
------------------------------------------------------------

Latest batch moved strict-core-only outer `do_wait()` process/thread scan
sequencing through Rust while preserving the C ABI wrapper, primitive
callbacks, and offset-based C fallback scaffolding:

- Parent children-lock callback ordering, children-list traversal, pid/pgid
  matching, `empty` publication, and no-found unlock/return sequencing.
- Process-zombie dispatch, process stopped/continued candidate dispatch, and
  ptraced-children empty-scan traversal.
- Report-thread list traversal, report-thread candidate dispatch, and regular
  thread-list empty-scan traversal.
- Equivalence coverage for process zombie dispatch, process candidate dispatch,
  ptraced-child empty scans, invalid empty pointers, report-thread release
  dispatch, and regular thread empty scans.

The Syscall core strict ownership row moves 90% -> 92% in `migration.txt`, and
the Process/VM strict ownership row moves 89% -> 90%. Post-+50 strict-core
movement is now about +207 points. The broad Syscall core and Process/VM
dashboard rows were already capped at 100%, so this does not add broad
dashboard percentage. Standalone readiness improves only slightly because
primitive lock/list/free bodies, waitqueue/schedule/signal primitive behavior,
host wait primitive behavior, syscall dispatch, user-copy primitive bodies,
default-path C primitive bodies, and fallback scaffolding remain C-owned.

Previous update 2026-05-26 wait-zombie/reparent strict-core batch

Previous batch moved strict-core-only `do_wait()` process-zombie sequencing

  selection.
- Parent rusage aggregation, rusage result fill, parent child-list detach, and
  PID-1 reparent/list attach sequencing.
- Child main-thread ptrace-detach gating, parent/child/PID-1/thread lock and
  unlock callback ordering, no-reparent unlock/detach ordering, process
  release callback dispatch, and final found/result shaping.
- Equivalence coverage for reparent, no-reparent, `WNOWAIT`, host wait
  failure, rusage copy, callback ordering, and invalid thread cases.

The Syscall core strict ownership row moved 89% -> 90% in `migration.txt`, and
the Process/VM strict ownership row moved 88% -> 89%. Post-+50 strict-core
movement was about +204 points. The broad Syscall core and Process/VM
dashboard rows were already capped at 100%, so this does not add broad
dashboard percentage. Standalone readiness improves only slightly because
outer child/thread traversal, lock/list/free primitive bodies, host wait

Previous update 2026-05-26 do_wait strict-core batch
------------------------------------------------------------

The previous batch moved strict-core-only top-level `do_wait()` wait-loop
sequencing through Rust while preserving the C ABI wrapper, typed primitive
callbacks, and C fallback scaffolding:

- Waitqueue entry initialization, prepare, and finish callback ordering.
- Process/thread scan dispatch gates using the existing `wait_proc()` and
  `wait_thread()` callbacks.
- No-child, `WNOHANG`, pending-signal interrupt, schedule handoff,
  wake/rescan, and wait-log sequencing.
- Equivalence coverage for immediate process hit, clone/thread scan, empty
  child set, `WNOHANG`, pending signal before sleep, sleep/rescan/found, and
  invalid callback/null cases.

The Syscall core strict ownership row moved 87% -> 88% in `migration.txt`.
Post-+50 strict-core movement was about +201 points. The broad Syscall core
dashboard row was already capped at 100%, so this did not add broad dashboard
percentage. Standalone readiness improved only slightly because child/thread
list traversal and mutation inside `wait_proc()`/`wait_thread()`, waitqueue,
schedule, signal, rusage, release/detach, syscall dispatch, user-copy
primitive bodies, default-path C primitive bodies, and fallback scaffolding
remained C-owned.

Previous 2026-05-26 scheduler migration-completion strict-core batch
------------------------------------------------------------

Previous batch moved strict-core-only `do_migrate()` migration-completion
sequencing through Rust while preserving the C ABI wrapper, typed primitive
callbacks, and C fallback scaffolding:

- Migration queue traversal with safe next-pointer capture.
- Request list detach plus already-migrated, current-CPU-allowed, empty
  affinity, and missing-target ack gates.
- Affinity target selection and CPU-local callback dispatch.
- Current/target runqueue lock ordering, runqueue detach/add, runqueue length
  mutation, and thread CPU publication.
- Same-VM sibling scan and address-space CPU-set lock/update sequencing.
- Target `CPU_FLAG_NEED_RESCHED` publication, interrupt/vector callback
  dispatch, migration log callback dispatch, waitqueue wake callback dispatch,
  and validation/error shaping.

The Scheduler/timers/wait/futex strict ownership row remains capped at 100% in
`migration.txt`, but this adds about +2 post-cap strict-core points. Post-+50
strict-core movement was about +200 points. The broad Scheduler/timers/
wait/futex dashboard row was already capped at 100%, so this does not add
broad dashboard percentage. Standalone readiness improves only slightly
because spinlock/waitqueue/interrupt/vector/log primitive bodies, CPU-local
lookup callbacks, context switching, `schedule()` primitive behavior,
default-path C primitive bodies, and fallback scaffolding remain C-owned.

scaffolding:

Update 2026-05-24 standalone Rust target tracking
------------------------------------------------------------

`migration.txt` now tracks a stricter standalone Rust end-state in addition to
the broad `overview.txt` dashboard and the strict-core ownership score. The
standalone target means the default x86_64/Rocky kernel and required
control-plane implementation are Rust-owned below the external/user-code
boundary. Other users can still write C or other language code on top of the OS,
and compatibility headers can remain, but McKernel-owned C execution bodies and
C fallback implementations should not be required underneath that boundary.

Current standalone Rust readiness is recorded as about 40% (roughly 35-45%).
It remains lower than strict-core ownership because many default-path C bodies,
fallbacks, wrappers, allocation/lifetime primitives, page-table mutation,
user-copy, scheduler/futex/signal side effects, IKC exchange, and object
lifecycle paths still need Rust ownership before the OS can fairly be called
standalone Rust.

Definition of "fully ported"
------------------------------------------------------------

The target end state is:

- McKernel-owned non-assembly implementation logic is Rust-owned.
- McKernel-adjacent IHK host modules, including `ihk`, `ihk-smp-x86_64`, and
  `mcctrl`, are in scope for the full port; until their Linux-module Rust
  build path is established, keep C shims and move pure logic behind
  C-callable Rust interfaces.
- Existing public C headers remain as compatibility surfaces while external
  users still depend on them.
- Rust replacement functions use the existing C ABI and exported symbol names.
- Architecture entry, interrupt, trampoline, and context-switch assembly remain
  assembly unless a later phase explicitly replaces them.
- Linux kernel internals and third-party code are not rewritten.
- Host/user components are ported where technically supportable by the target
  kernel and toolchain. If external Rust Linux modules are not supported, keep a
  small C module shim and move pure logic behind C-callable Rust interfaces.

Completed migration work
------------------------------------------------------------

Rust kernel build foundation
----------------------------

- Added a freestanding Rust kernel crate under kernel/rust/.
- Added no_std Rust code with panic abort behavior and no allocator dependency.
- Wired the Rust object into the x86_64 mckernel.img link when
  ENABLE_RUST_KERNEL is enabled.
- Preserved a C fallback path when ENABLE_RUST_KERNEL is disabled.
- Restricted Rust enablement to the current x86_64 migration target.
- Configured the Rust build for kernel-style code generation:
  no red zone, large code model, static relocation model, frame pointers, no
  vectorization, no unwinding, and no allocator.
- Updated the x86_64 linker script to include large-model Rust text sections.

ABI and layout foundation
-------------------------

- Added Rust ABI definitions in kernel/rust/abi.rs.
- Added C-side ABI/layout checks in kernel/rust/abi_checks.c.
- Covered the first shared syscall/protocol structures needed by the migration.
- Added ABI/layout checks for `struct rb_node`, `struct rb_root`, and
  `struct free_chunk` before moving page-allocator helpers to Rust.
- Added ABI/layout checks for `struct list_head`, `mcs_lock_node_t`, and
  `struct ihk_page_allocator_desc` before moving the bitmap-backed page
  allocator internals to Rust.
- Added ABI/layout checks for `struct memobj`, `struct ipc_perm`,
  `struct shmid_ds`, and `struct shmobj` before moving SysV shared-memory
  helper policy to Rust.
- Added Rust ABI mirrors plus C/Rust layout checks for `cpu_set_t`,
  `mcs_rwlock_lock_t`, `struct process_hash`, `struct thread_hash`,
  `struct address_space`, `struct vm_range`, `struct vm_range_numa_policy`,
  `struct vm_regions`, and `struct process_vm` before moving more process/VM
  and x86_64 memory-management bodies to Rust.
- Added Rust ABI mirrors plus C/Rust layout checks for pager create/map result
  packets and McKernel memory-control structures: `struct ihk_mc_memory_area`,
  `struct ihk_mc_memory_node`, `struct ihk_mc_pa_ops`,
  `struct tlb_flush_entry`, and `struct ihk_mc_page_cache_header`.
- Preserved public C headers, symbol names, syscall numbers, ioctl values, IKC
  packet formats, and linker-visible entry points.

First Rust primitive replacements
---------------------------------

- Ported the kernel red-black tree implementation behind the existing rb_* C
  ABI.
- Ported linked-list helper routines behind the existing C ABI.
- Ported priority-list add/delete helpers behind the existing C ABI:
  `plist_add()` and `plist_del()`.
- Ported the pure waitqueue locked list core behind the existing C ABI:
  `waitq_init()`, `waitq_add_entry_locked()`,
  `waitq_remove_entry_locked()`, and `waitq_wake_nr_locked()`. Scheduler
  callbacks, spinlock wrappers, current-thread state transitions, wakeup
  wrappers, timers, and futex logic remain C-owned.
- Ported the small init/memory query helper cluster behind the existing C ABI:
  `find_command_line()` from `kernel/init.c`, plus
  `is_mckernel_memory()` and `phys_to_nid()` from `kernel/mem.c`. The page
  address validator, page-table/mapping code, and high-risk memory management
  behavior remain C-owned.
- Ported the process/VM decision-helper batch behind the existing C ABI:
  `common_vrflag_to_ptattr()`, `process_split_pgshift_result()`,
  `process_add_range_bounds_result()`, `process_extend_up_result()`,
  `process_change_prot_newflag_result()`, `process_attr_delta_result()`,
  `process_private_file_setattr_result()`,
  `process_remove_region_alignment_result()`,
  `process_access_initial_result()`, `process_access_adjacent_result()`, and
  `process_access_permission_result()`. Allocation, rb-tree mutation, locks,
  page-table mutation, fault handling, range lifetime, and logging remain
  C-owned.
- Ported the x86_64 page-attribute translation helper batch behind the
  existing C ABI: `x86_attr_to_l3attr_result()`,
  `x86_attr_to_l2attr_result()`, and `x86_attr_to_l1attr_result()`. The
  wrappers `attr_to_l3attr()`, `attr_to_l2attr()`, and `attr_to_l1attr()` now
  call Rust when ENABLE_RUST_KERNEL is on. Page-table walking, mapping,
  unmapping, TLB-sensitive mutation, and user-copy remain C-owned.
- Ported the x86_64 smaller-page-size selection helper behind the existing C
  ABI: `x86_smaller_page_size_result()`. `arch_get_smaller_page_size()` now
  calls Rust when ENABLE_RUST_KERNEL is on. Page-table splitting, page-table
  walking, TLB flushes, and mapping mutation remain C-owned.
- Ported the page query/splitability helper cluster behind the existing C ABI:
  `page_to_phys()` and `is_splitable()` from `kernel/mem.c`. Page hash lookup,
  page-table walking, mapping/unmapping, and memory-object operations remain
  C-owned.
- Ported the SysV shared-memory ID helper cluster behind the existing C ABI:
  `get_shmid_max_index()`, `get_shmid_index()`, `make_shmid()`,
  `shmid_to_index()`, and `shmid_to_seq()` from `kernel/syscall.c`.
- Ported the SysV shared-memory permission and memory-lock rlimit helper
  cluster behind the existing C ABI: `shmget_existing_access_result()`,
  `shmat_access_result()`, `shmctl_ipc_stat_access_result()`,
  `shm_owner_result()`, `shm_owner_or_cap_result()`, and
  `shmlock_rlimit_result()`. Shared-memory object lookup, locking, reference
  handling, user-copy, mapping, and syscall orchestration remain C-owned.
- Ported the scheduler priority policy helper pair behind the existing C ABI:
  `sched_get_priority_max_value()` and `sched_get_priority_min_value()` from
  `kernel/syscall.c`. The syscall wrappers still extract arguments in C, and
  scheduler/thread lookup behavior remains C-owned.
- Ported additional scheduler policy and affinity helpers behind the existing
  C ABI: `sched_policy_is_valid()`, `sched_policy_needs_root()`,
  `setscheduler_validate()`, `sched_rr_interval_nsec()`,
  `sched_affinity_permission_result()`, `sched_getaffinity_len_result()`, and
  `sched_affinity_copy_len()`. Thread lookup, Linux permission forwarding,
  mask mutation, migration requests, and user-copy remain C-owned.
- Ported `prlimit64` resource policy helpers behind the existing C ABI:
  `prlimit_validate_resource()`, `prlimit_validate_new_limit()`,
  `prlimit_linux_update_needed()`, and `prlimit_to_mckernel_resource()`.
  Process lookup, lock ownership, user-copy, rlimit storage, and Linux
  forwarding remain C-owned.
- Ported syscall policy and VM range-validation helpers behind the existing C
  ABI: `robust_list_len_result()`, `tkill_tid_result()`,
  `sigaction_validate()`, `setpgid_normalize_pid()`,
  `setpgid_normalize_pgid()`, `setpgid_execed_result()`,
  `memlock_prepare_range()`, `memlock_range_flag_result()`,
  `range_has_disallowed_change_flags()`, `munmap_prepare_range()`, and
  `mprotect_prepare_range()`. Signal/action state, process lookup, VM range
  tree mutation, page-table changes, locking, and user-copy remain C-owned.
- Ported additional memory-syscall and NUMA-policy validation helpers behind
  the existing C ABI: `mlockall_policy_result()`,
  `remap_file_pages_prepare()`, `mremap_prepare_args()`,
  `mremap_fixed_range_result()`, `mremap_maymove_result()`,
  `msync_prepare_range()`, `msync_locked_range_result()`,
  `mbind_prepare_range()`, `mempolicy_nodemask_bits_result()`,
  `mempolicy_nodemask_bits_is_clamped()`, `mbind_mode_flags_result()`,
  `mempolicy_mode_is_supported()`, `set_mempolicy_normalize_mode()`,
  `get_mempolicy_validate()`, and `move_pages_policy_result()`. Mapping
  mutation, NUMA mask copy/verification, migration allocation, page-table
  updates, and range-tree ownership remain C-owned.
- Ported additional `brk()`, `mincore()`, and `do_mmap()` policy helpers
  behind the existing C ABI: `brk_prepare_result()`,
  `brk_default_vrflags()`, `mincore_prepare_range()`,
  `mmap_base_vrflags()`, `mmap_populated_mapping_result()`,
  `mmap_should_set_host_ro()`, `mmap_update_private_maxprot()`,
  `mmap_prot_denied_result()`, `mmap_maxprot_to_vrflags()`,
  `mmap_should_force_straight()`, and `mmap_is_shared()`. Free-space search,
  memory-object creation, physical page allocation, host VMA updates, range
  insertion, prefaulting, and mapping teardown remain C-owned.
- Ported additional signal/time syscall policy helpers behind the existing C
  ABI: `tgkill_target_result()`, `rt_sigprocmask_validate()`,
  `rt_sigprocmask_apply()`, `rt_sigpending_size_result()`,
  `signalfd4_sigsetsize_result()`, `signalfd4_flags_result()`,
  `getrusage_who_result()`, `itimer_which_result()`, `itimer_is_real()`,
  `itimer_should_start()`, `clock_gettime_dispatch()`,
  `gettimeofday_dispatch()`, and `nanosleep_validate_timespec()`. Signal
  queues, user-copy, timer state mutation, CPU-time accounting walks, and
  Linux forwarding remain C-owned.
- Ported additional signal-wait and alternate-signal-stack policy helpers
  behind the existing C ABI: `rt_sigtimedwait_prepare()`,
  `rt_sigtimedwait_timeout_result()`, `rt_sigtimedwait_prepare_masks()`,
  `rt_sigtimedwait_deadline()`, `rt_sigtimedwait_timeout_expired()`,
  `sigmask_to_signal_number()`, `rt_sigqueueinfo_pid_result()`,
  `sigsuspend_sigsetsize_result()`, `sigsuspend_prepare_mask()`,
  `sigsuspend_pending_matches()`, `sigaction_sigsetsize_result()`,
  `sigaltstack_validate()`, and `sigaltstack_is_disable()`. Pending-signal
  list traversal, signal delivery, action storage, thread state transitions,
  alternate-stack storage updates, and user-copy remain C-owned.
- Ported ptrace and process-vm policy helpers behind the existing C ABI:
  `process_vm_validate_args()`, `process_vm_op_is_write()`,
  `process_vm_op_is_valid()`, `ptrace_signal_data_result()`,
  `ptrace_detach_signal_result()`, `ptrace_user_area_result()`,
  `ptrace_status_allows_io()`, `ptrace_setoptions_flags_result()`,
  `ptrace_apply_options()`, `ptrace_child_traced_result()`,
  `ptrace_attach_policy_result()`, `ptrace_detach_state_result()`, and
  `ptrace_siginfo_state_result()`. Process lookup, ptrace state mutation,
  register access, remote memory reads/writes, cross-process VM copying, and
  user-copy remain C-owned.
- Ported wait4/waitid process/thread wait policy helpers behind the existing
  C ABI: `wait4_options_result()`, `waitid_to_wait_pid_result()`,
  `waitid_options_result()`, `wait_should_scan_process_result()`,
  `wait_should_scan_thread_result()`, `wait_process_pid_matches_result()`,
  `wait_thread_tid_matches_result()`, `wait_process_exited_candidate_result()`,
  `wait_thread_exited_candidate_result()`,
  `wait_nonptraced_stop_candidate_result()`,
  `wait_ptraced_stop_candidate_result()`,
  `wait_continued_candidate_result()`, `wait_reap_needed_result()`,
  `wait_nohang_result()`, `wait_empty_result()`,
  `wait_stopped_status_result()`, `wait_continued_status_result()`,
  `wait_zombie_skip_host_result()`, `wait_thread_empty_candidate_result()`,
  and `waitid_status_code_result()`. Child-list traversal/mutation,
  reparenting, rusage aggregation, signal delivery, ptrace detach mutation,
  host wait cleanup, and user-copy remain C-owned.
- Ported clone, execveat, ptrace-event, and futex command policy helpers
  behind the existing C ABI: `clone_pthread_marker_result()`,
  `clone_flags_result()`, `clone_host_parent_flags_result()`,
  `clone_report_thread_result()`, `ptrace_exec_event_signal_result()`,
  `ptrace_syscall_event_signal_result()`, `ptrace_clone_event_result()`,
  `ptrace_clone_reparent_result()`, `execveat_policy_result()`,
  `futex_decode_flags_result()`, `futex_wait_timeout_needed_result()`,
  `futex_timeout_is_absolute_result()`, `futex_clock_id_result()`,
  `futex_requeue_val2_result()`, and `futex_timeout_ns_result()`. Thread and
  process allocation, ptrace report mutation, exec image loading, futex queue
  operations, futex wake/requeue internals, Linux clock/syscall fallback, and
  user-copy remain C-owned.
- Ported XPMEM policy helpers behind the existing C ABI:
  `xpmem_make_initial_policy_result()`, `xpmem_make_alignment_result()`,

  `xpmem_attach_overlap_result()`.
- Ported additional XPMEM ID, hash, owner, attach/detach, and
  destroying-state policy helpers behind the existing C ABI:
  `xpmem_id_to_tgid_result()`, `xpmem_tg_hashtable_index_result()`,

  `xpmem_two_destroying_error_result()`,
  `xpmem_three_destroying_error_result()`,
  `xpmem_attach_destroying_result()`, and
  `xpmem_detach_lookup_result()`. XPMEM object allocation, lock ownership,
  refcount mutation, list mutation, remap/page-table updates, and user-copy
  remain C-owned.
- Ported additional XPMEM range and page-table decision helpers behind the
  existing C ABI: `xpmem_remove_range_step_result()`,
  `xpmem_remove_memory_range_action_result()`,
  `xpmem_range_private_invalid_result()`,
  `xpmem_clear_pte_range_result()`, `xpmem_fault_vaddr_result()`,
  `xpmem_straight_phys_result()`, `xpmem_remote_pte_missing_result()`,

  `xpmem_pte_mismatch_result()`, and `xpmem_unpin_step_result()`. Range
  splitting/freeing, attachment list mutation, page-table mutation, PTE
  installation, pin/unpin accounting mutation, and user-copy remain C-owned.
- Ported additional XPMEM refcount/object-list and destroy-state decision
  helpers behind the existing C ABI: `xpmem_close_decision_result()`,
  `xpmem_ref_drop_should_free_result()`,
  `xpmem_begin_destroy_result()`, `xpmem_finish_destroy_result()`, and
  `xpmem_object_lookup_decision_result()`. Atomic reference mutation, frees,
  lock/list mutation, attach/remap/page-table mutation, and user-copy remain
  C-owned.
- Ported the first file/dev/procfs/sysfs object decision helper batch behind
  the existing C ABI through `kernel/rust/object_helpers.rs` and the C fallback
  in `kernel/object_helpers.c`. Rust now owns file-object hash/mode/refcount
  keep/base-flag/status/premap/page-I/O/flush decisions; device-object
  page-count, PFN-table, PFN-cache, PFN-present, physical-address, and
  range-index decisions; sysfs path validation, special-op classification,
  string-bit sizing, and response-error mapping; and procfs memory-fault
  reason, chunk sizing, pagemap range, process-status state, and thread-stat
  state selection. Allocation, locks, list mutation, page-table mutation,
  page I/O, sysfs IKC exchange, procfs buffer mutation, and user-copy remain
  C-owned.
- Ported the second object-helper decision batch behind the same C ABI.
  Rust now owns pager EINTR retry, scratch allocation sizing, copy-size
  validation, fault-address masking, and read chunk sizing; zeroobj initial
  flags/refcount and page validation; SysV shmobj pgshift/pgsize, flags,
  real-size, page containment, destroy-index, get/lookup-page, and update-page
  address decisions; and hugefile object p2align, page-index, per-page count,
  pgsize, initial status/refcount, grow/copy/zero sizing decisions.
  Allocation, reference mutation, locks, lists, page allocation/free,
  page-table mutation, user-copy, and object lifetime mutation remain C-owned.
- Ported the third object/procfs/sysfs/dev/file decision-helper batch behind
  the same C ABI. Rust now owns fileobj initial ref/sref, premap NUMA-node
  wrap, pages-byte sizing, premap page indexing, allocation npages/flags/size,
  pageio pgsize and reschedule decisions, and new/mapped page modes; devobj
  PFN table bytes, base flags, initial refcount, PFN request offset, PFN-store
  decision, and map size; sysfs parameter sizing, data buffer sizing, and
  packet-error decisions; and procfs default/remote counts, remote npages,
  format-error validation, locked-KB reporting, maps permission chars, maps
  path kind, pagemap next position, and auxv limit. Allocation, reference
  mutation, locks, lists, page-table mutation, sysfs IKC exchange, procfs
  buffer mutation, and user-copy remain C-owned.
- Ported the fourth object/procfs/sysfs/dev/file decision-helper batch behind
  the same C ABI. Rust now owns fileobj path-present, invalid page-count,
  hashed-page free, premap page-present, and lookup-miss decisions; devobj
  path/PFN-table presence and mapped-PFN composition; sysfs request-busy,
  handle-pointer, default response, release-response, and request-handler
  dispatch decisions; and procfs release-message, root match, OS-number match,
  zero-length read, locked-size accumulation, and bitmask offset decisions.
  Allocation/free calls, reference mutation, locks, lists, page-table
  mutation, sysfs IKC sends, procfs buffer mutation, and user-copy remain
  C-owned.
- Ported the exported bitops scan/hweight helper cluster behind the existing C
  ABI: `find_next_bit()`, `find_next_zero_bit()`, `find_first_bit()`,
  `find_first_zero_bit()`, and the `__sw_hweight*()` helpers.
- Ported the external string/memory leaf helper cluster behind the existing C
  ABI: `strlen()`, `strnlen()`, `strcpy()`, `strncpy()`, `strcmp()`,
  `strncmp()`, `strchr()`, `strrchr()`, `strpbrk()`, `strstr()`, `memcpy()`,
  `memcpy_long()`, `memset()`, and `memcmp()`. Left
  `flatten_strings_from_user()` in C because it depends on allocation and

- Ported the numeric parser leaf helper cluster behind the existing C ABI:
  `simple_strtoul()`, `simple_strtol()`, `simple_strtoull()`,
  `simple_strtoll()`, `strict_strtoul()`, `strict_strtol()`,
  `strict_strtoull()`, `strict_strtoll()`, and `strtol()`. Left varargs
  formatting and scanning in C.
- Ported selected bitmap primitives behind the existing C ABI.
- Ported bitmap hex/list parser helpers behind the existing C ABI:
  `__bitmap_parse()`, `bitmap_parse_user()`, `bitmap_parselist()`, and

  depends on `scnprintf()`.
- Ported bitmap_find_next_zero_area() behind the existing C ABI.
- Ported the bitmap region helper cluster behind the existing C ABI:
  bitmap_find_free_region(), bitmap_allocate_region(), and
  bitmap_release_region().
- Ported the bitmap remap helper cluster behind the existing C ABI:
  bitmap_ord_to_pos(), bitmap_remap(), bitmap_bitremap(), bitmap_onto(),

- Started the page allocator phase by porting the rbtree helper cluster
  `__page_alloc_rbtree_free_range()` and
  `__page_alloc_rbtree_alloc_pages()` behind the existing C ABI.
- Continued the page allocator phase by porting the generic bitmap-backed
  no-lock helper cluster behind C wrappers:
  `__ihk_pagealloc_large_nolock()`, `__ihk_pagealloc_alloc_nolock()`,

- Extended the page allocator Rust phase by porting the no-lock zero-free
  helper behind the exported C lock/log wrapper:
  `__ihk_pagealloc_zero_free_pages_nolock()`.
- Extended the rbtree allocator helper set with Rust-owned
  `__page_alloc_rbtree_reserve_pages()` and
  `__page_alloc_rbtree_get_root_chunk()`.
- Moved the NUMA add-free helper body to Rust behind the exported C logging

- Moved the single-node NUMA deferred-zero helper to Rust behind the C
  all-node wrapper: `__ihk_numa_zero_free_pages_node()`.
- Moved the under-lock NUMA allocation body to Rust behind the exported C

- Moved the direct NUMA free-to-tree helper to Rust behind the exported C

- Moved the deferred NUMA free enqueue helper to Rust while keeping the

- Moved NUMA free-path preflight and action selection to Rust:
  `__ihk_numa_free_pages_prepare()` now owns bounds checking, positive page

- Kept macro-heavy C headers in place so existing C call sites do not need a

- Guarded selected C bitmap implementations so Rust can replace them without
  duplicate symbols.

  branch.

Known current validation state
------------------------------------------------------------

- WSL is useful for development and partial builds, but not the preferred
  runtime boot environment.
- Full WSL module/runtime validation remains blocked by host kernel/module
  compatibility problems, especially the ihk-smp-x86_64 driver against Linux
  6.6 APIs.
- Rocky Linux VM validation is the preferred runtime path.
- Current Rust migration is healthy through the Rust primitive and first
  allocator-helper equivalence layer, mckernel.img build layer, host module
  compile layer, module-load layer, Rocky VM boot-only layer, V10 minimal
  workload layer, and focused V11 Rocky gate with required LTP/XPMEM.
- V9 McKernel boot-only validation passed on Rocky VM.
- V10 is stable at the current minimal workload level. The latest completed
  rerun after the Rust SysV shared-memory permission/helper batch was the
  sudo-authenticated non-root Rocky command on 2026-05-18 at 04:20 EDT:
  `scripts/rocky-rust-validation.sh --skip-deps --skip-rust --boot-smoke --trace-smoke --yes --jobs 2`.
  It passed `mcexec /bin/true`, relative `mcexec hostname`, absolute
  `mcexec /usr/bin/hostname`, and `mcstat`, then shut down cleanly.
- Latest V10 log after the Rust NUMA free-to-tree and deferred-free enqueue
  helper ports:
  `/tmp/mckernel-v10-20260518-014825.log`.
- The previous hostname SIGSEGV at `RIP=0x1000` is fixed by explicit zero-fill
  in user-visible mmap/page-fault paths.
- Preserve this exact V10 rerun command for future baseline checks:
  `scripts/rocky-rust-validation.sh --skip-deps --skip-rust --boot-smoke --trace-smoke --yes --jobs 2`
- Save each full V10 log as
  `/tmp/mckernel-v10-YYYYMMDD-HHMMSS.log`.

  issue-1324 fork/ptrace pressure, plus an x86_64 mcexec-options suite because
  only arm64 mcexec-options tests are present in this tree.
- The persistent GRUB memmap trampoline experiment is unsafe on the current
  Rocky/VirtualBox setup. Do not use persistent memmap changes as the default
  validation path.

Public interface rules
------------------------------------------------------------

- Preserve syscall numbers.
- Preserve ioctl values.
- Preserve IKC packet formats.
- Preserve exported symbol names.
- Preserve public C headers during the migration.
- Use extern "C" for Rust functions replacing C implementations.
- Use #[repr(C)] for all shared ABI structs.
- Keep union-heavy and flexible-array-heavy interfaces pointer/raw-layout based
  until each call site is deliberately migrated.
- Do not make behavior-breaking ABI changes without a separate approved plan.

Controlled verification ladder
------------------------------------------------------------

Run the steps in order. Stop on the first failure. Do not proceed to runtime
checks until all earlier build and equivalence checks pass.

V0: Documentation integrity
---------------------------

Purpose:
- Confirm this documentation update is structurally clean.

Commands:
- test -f plan.txt
- test -f overview.txt
- test -f kernel.log
- git diff --check

Success:
- All three files exist.
- git diff --check reports no whitespace or patch-format problems.

Stop condition:
- If a file is missing or diff hygiene fails, fix documentation before testing
  code.

V1: Source inventory
--------------------

Purpose:
- Confirm the repo still contains both Rust and C migration paths.

Commands:
- find kernel/rust -maxdepth 2 -type f | sort
- grep or rg for ENABLE_RUST_KERNEL in kernel/CMakeLists.txt
- confirm kernel/rbtree.c, kernel/llist.c, and lib/bitmap.c still exist
- confirm kernel/rust/lib.rs, abi.rs, rbtree.rs, llist.rs, waitq.rs,
  plist.rs, bitops.rs, string.rs, numparse.rs, bitmap.rs, page_alloc.rs,
  mem_helpers.rs, and abi_checks.c still exist

Success:
- Rust files are present.
- C fallback files are present.
- ENABLE_RUST_KERNEL is still available.

Stop condition:
- If either Rust or C fallback inventory is missing, fix source layout before
  building.

V2: Rust object only
--------------------

Purpose:
- Compile Rust before touching the full kernel link.

Command:
- cmake --build <build-dir> --target mckernel_rust_obj -j2

Additional checks:
- Confirm no allocator dependency.
- Confirm no unwinding dependency.
- Confirm no red-zone usage.
- Confirm no unexpected SIMD/vector instructions.

Success:
- The Rust object target builds cleanly.

Stop condition:
- If this fails, debug Rust flags, nightly availability, or no_std code before
  trying mckernel.img.

V3: Rust primitive equivalence
------------------------------

Purpose:
- Confirm Rust replacements match the C behavior already being replaced.

Command:
- kernel/rust/tests/run_equivalence.sh

Success:
- rbtree equivalence passes.
- llist equivalence passes.
- waitq-core equivalence passes.
- memory/init helper equivalence passes.
- page query/splitability helper equivalence passes.
- SysV shmid and shared-memory permission helper equivalence passes.
- scheduler priority, policy, and affinity helper equivalence passes.
- prlimit helper equivalence passes.
- plist equivalence passes.
- bitops equivalence passes.
- string/memory equivalence passes.
- numeric parser equivalence passes.
- bitmap equivalence passes.
- bitmap parser equivalence passes.
- page allocator helper equivalence passes.
- unresolved-symbol checks pass.
- codegen checks pass.

Stop condition:
- If this fails, fix the relevant primitive before attempting a kernel image
  build.

V4: Rust-enabled kernel image
-----------------------------

Purpose:
- Confirm Rust links into mckernel.img.

Configure:
- cmake -S . -B <rust-build-dir> -DBUILD_TARGET=smp-x86 -DENABLE_RUST_KERNEL=ON

Build:
- cmake --build <rust-build-dir> --target mckernel.img -j2

Success:
- mckernel.img links with the Rust object included.

Stop condition:
- If this fails, debug link script, duplicate symbols, Rust object generation,
  or missing kernel symbols.

V5: C fallback kernel image
---------------------------

Purpose:
- Confirm the old C path remains usable.

Configure:
- cmake -S . -B <c-build-dir> -DBUILD_TARGET=smp-x86 -DENABLE_RUST_KERNEL=OFF

Build:
- cmake --build <c-build-dir> --target mckernel.img -j2

Success:
- mckernel.img links without Rust replacements.

Stop condition:
- If this fails, fix fallback source selection before porting more code.

V6: User-space tools
--------------------

Purpose:
- Confirm user-space tools still compile before module or runtime work.

Build:
- cmake --build <build-dir> --target mcexec mcstat -j2

Success:
- mcexec and mcstat build.

Stop condition:
- If this fails, debug host/user library or libbfd/libelf dependencies before
  module loading.

V7: Host modules compile
------------------------

Purpose:
- Confirm host modules are buildable on the selected runtime host.

Build:
- cmake --build <build-dir> --target ihk_ko ihk-smp-x86_64_ko mcctrl_ko -j2

Preferred environment:
- Rocky Linux VM or another compatible full Linux kernel development
  environment.

Success:
- ihk.ko, ihk-smp-x86_64.ko, and mcctrl.ko build.

Stop condition:
- If this fails, do not attempt runtime boot. Fix Linux kernel API/module
  compatibility first.

V8: Module load only
--------------------

Purpose:
- Confirm Linux accepts the modules before booting McKernel.

Action:
- Load the required modules.
- Do not boot McKernel yet.

Success:
- Required modules load without unresolved symbols, taint surprises that block
  operation, or immediate kernel errors.

Stop condition:
- If module load fails, debug module dependencies and dmesg before boot.
- A dmesg warning that IHK-SMP fell back to Linux's trampoline on the
  Rocky/VirtualBox VM is not a V8 failure if all modules load and remain
  healthy.
- Do not add persistent GRUB/kernel command-line `memmap` reservations to clear
  this warning.

V9: McKernel boot only
----------------------

Purpose:
- Confirm host modules and mckernel.img cooperate at boot.

Action:
- Boot with minimal CPU and memory allocation.
- Do not run user workloads yet.
- Use the default Linux-trampoline fallback on Rocky/VirtualBox unless it causes
  a boot failure.
- Do not mutate persistent boot arguments for trampoline experiments during V9.

Success:
- Kernel message output confirms McKernel booted.

Stop condition:
- If boot fails, inspect host dmesg and McKernel kmsg before testing mcexec.
- If boot fails while destroying OS instance 0, first clean stale state with
  `mcstop+release.sh -k`; if the stale instance still cannot be destroyed,
  reboot the Rocky VM before retrying.

V10: Minimal user workloads
---------------------------

Purpose:
- Confirm basic process launch and reporting.

Commands:
- mcexec /bin/true
- mcexec hostname
- mcexec /usr/bin/hostname
- mcstat

Repeatability command:
- `scripts/rocky-rust-validation.sh --skip-deps --skip-rust --boot-smoke --trace-smoke --yes --jobs 2`

Log capture:
- Save the full terminal log under
  `/tmp/mckernel-v10-YYYYMMDD-HHMMSS.log`.
- Keep per-command smoke logs in `/tmp/mckernel-<label>.out` as short-term
  diagnostics only; the full run log is the artifact to preserve.

Notes:
- The hostname smoke compares relative and absolute argv0 forms.
- The latest clean repeat is `/tmp/mckernel-v10-20260517-191408.log`.
- Earlier `RIP=0x1000` hostname failures in
  `/tmp/mckernel-v10-20260516-211721.log` are historical regression evidence,
  not the current boundary.

Success:
- `/bin/true`, relative `hostname`, absolute `/usr/bin/hostname`, and `mcstat`
  complete successfully.

Stop condition:
- If any command fails, debug process/syscall/host communication paths before
  broad regression tests.
- On any regression, stop after the first failure and classify the handoff by
  the last marker reached: prepared process, schedule message received, queued
  on run queue, scheduler switch, final user entry, syscall entry, user page
  fault, user exception, first delegated syscall sent, signal/default
  termination, or fatal page-fault registers.
- After a V10 watchdog failure, use kill-in-use cleanup before retrying so the
  next run does not fail at V9 cleanup because a stale `mcexec` still has
  `/dev/mcos0` open.
- The harness should print the watched smoke PID immediately. If that line does
  not appear, debug the harness launch path; if it appears and no timeout
  follows, collect state from a second terminal and treat the watchdog itself as
  blocked.

V11: Focused regression tests
-----------------------------

Purpose:
- Exercise high-risk functionality touched by the migration.

Suites:
- user_space/futex
- large_page/x86_64
- xpmem
- strace
- mcexec_options
- syscall delegation tests

Current strace/ptrace status on Rocky:
- Issue 945 now passes end to end after saving the original execve user
  context for synthetic execve syscall-exit reporting and waking main-thread
  ptrace stops directly as `PS_TRACED`.
- Issues 943, 944, 946, 960, and 961 now pass with zero internal NG counts
  after updating Rocky expectations for `openat` and modern strace output.
- Direct `strace /bin/true`, `strace -e trace=none /bin/true`,
  `strace -e trace=exit_group /bin/true`, `strace -d -e trace=none
  /bin/true`, and `strace -d -o ... /bin/true` complete successfully under
  `mcexec`.
- Bundle tests `ptrace_setoptions`, `qual_syscall`, `stat`, and `strace-f`
  pass. The old `stat`/`strace-f` panic is fixed by avoiding the missing Rocky
  host `vgtod_virt`; the user VDSO is disabled on this path and `SYS_time`
  stays local through the boot-time/TSC fallback.
- `scripts/rocky-v11-focused.sh --timeout 120` reruns this focused strace
  slice from a Rocky-installed prefix instead of relying on older hardcoded
  `mic/mcexec` or user-home paths.

Current non-strace V11 status on Rocky:
- Runnable local futex probes pass:
  `C1176T03`, `C1176T04`, and `test/portability/futex_wake_op`.
- Runnable robust-list probe `C1399T01` passes.
- Runnable syscall-time probe `CT_001` passes, and the host strace summary for
  that run contains no Linux `time` syscall from `mcexec`.
- Runnable issue-1324 small-VM subcases pass:
  `C1324T03` (`/proc/self/mem`) and `C1324T02` (`sigaltstack`).
- Issue-1324 `C1324T01` remains classified as not runnable on this VM size: it
  returns `ENOMEM` at `fork()` before reaching ptrace with 1536M, 2048M, and
  1536M plus `-O` McKernel boot profiles.
- `test/large_page/x86_64` builds all 4K binaries locally:
  `001_4K`, `101_4K`, `201_4K`, `301_4K`, `401_4K`, `402_4K`, `501_4K`,
  `601_4K`.
- Full LTP-backed `user_space/futex` and syscall delegation tests are blocked
  until `~/ltp/testcases/bin` exists with the needed LTP binaries.
- Full `xpmem` is blocked until the XPMEM module/userspace stack is installed
  for `4.18.0-553.el8_10.x86_64`.
- Full `mcexec_options` is blocked because this checkout has only
  `test/mcexec_options/arm64`; add or recover an x86_64 suite before calling
  that V11 item complete on Rocky x86_64.
- The focused Rocky runner classifies these missing prerequisites explicitly
  and exits successfully only after the locally runnable coverage passes.

Success:
- Targeted suites pass.

Stop condition:
- If a suite fails, stop at that subsystem and add a phase-specific repro.
- Keep future V11 repros bounded with `timeout`, preserve the full command log,
  and inspect recent McKernel kmsg before making broader subsystem changes.

V12: Phase-specific stress
--------------------------

Purpose:
- Keep future verification narrow and debuggable.

Rule:
- Each Rust port phase gets tests specific to the code just moved.

Examples:
- Allocator phase: allocation/free/coalescing stress before syscall tests.
- Page-table phase: mapping/unmapping/attribute tests before process tests.
- Futex phase: futex stress before file-object tests.

Success:
- The new Rust phase passes its specific stress tests, then passes V4 through
  V11.

Stop condition:
- If stress fails, do not port the next subsystem.

Granular migration phases
------------------------------------------------------------

Phase 0: Documentation and Baseline
-----------------------------------

Scope:
- Create plan.txt and overview.txt.
- Update kernel.log with this documentation change.
- Confirm current branch, build status, known blockers, and Rocky VM validation
  path.
- Record that verification is now staged by V0 through V12.

Exit criteria:
- Docs accurately describe completed work and current limitations.
- V0 passes.
- V1 passes if source inventory is checked during the same work session.

Phase 1: Rust build foundation hardening
----------------------------------------

Scope:
- Improve CMake diagnostics for missing nightly rustc.
- Make the Rust target name and output path obvious.
- Ensure Rust build flags remain centralized and auditable.
- Keep ENABLE_RUST_KERNEL=ON and ENABLE_RUST_KERNEL=OFF equally supported.

Exit criteria:
- V1 through V5 pass.

Phase 2: ABI foundation expansion
---------------------------------

Scope:
- Expand Rust #[repr(C)] mirrors for syscall, IKC, process, memory, and x86_64
  context structures.
- Add C layout assertions for every mirrored type.
- Add offset and size checks where padding is security- or ABI-sensitive.

Exit criteria:
- ABI checks fail on intentional layout drift.
- V2 through V5 pass.

Phase 3: Shared primitive completion
------------------------------------

Scope:
- Finish bitmap migration.
- Port additional leaf primitives only where the C ABI is stable.
- Keep C macro headers intact.
- Avoid introducing allocator-backed Rust data structures.
- Current completed slices:
  - rbtree and llist helpers are Rust-owned.
  - plist add/delete helpers are Rust-owned.
  - waitqueue locked list core helpers are Rust-owned.
  - bitops scan/hweight helpers are Rust-owned.
  - numeric parser leaf helpers are Rust-owned.
  - bitmap core, zero-area, region, and remap helper clusters are Rust-owned.
  - bitmap parse helpers are Rust-owned.
  - string/memory leaf helpers from `lib/string.c` are Rust-owned, while
    `flatten_strings_from_user()` remains C-owned.
  - small init/memory query helpers are Rust-owned:
    `find_command_line()`, `is_mckernel_memory()`, and `phys_to_nid()`.
  - page query/splitability helpers are Rust-owned:
    `page_to_phys()` and `is_splitable()`.
  - SysV shmid helpers are Rust-owned:
    `get_shmid_max_index()`, `get_shmid_index()`, `make_shmid()`,
    `shmid_to_index()`, and `shmid_to_seq()`.
  - SysV shared-memory permission and rlimit helpers are Rust-owned:
    `shmget_existing_access_result()`, `shmat_access_result()`,
    `shmctl_ipc_stat_access_result()`, `shm_owner_result()`,
    `shm_owner_or_cap_result()`, and `shmlock_rlimit_result()`.
  - scheduler priority min/max policy helpers are Rust-owned:
    `sched_get_priority_max_value()` and `sched_get_priority_min_value()`.
  - scheduler policy, RR interval, and affinity permission/length helpers are
    Rust-owned:
    `sched_policy_is_valid()`, `sched_policy_needs_root()`,
    `setscheduler_validate()`, `sched_rr_interval_nsec()`,
    `sched_affinity_permission_result()`, `sched_getaffinity_len_result()`,
    and `sched_affinity_copy_len()`.
  - prlimit resource policy helpers are Rust-owned:
    `prlimit_validate_resource()`, `prlimit_validate_new_limit()`,
    `prlimit_linux_update_needed()`, and
    `prlimit_to_mckernel_resource()`.
  - syscall policy and VM range-validation helpers are Rust-owned:
    `robust_list_len_result()`, `tkill_tid_result()`,
    `sigaction_validate()`, `setpgid_normalize_pid()`,
    `setpgid_normalize_pgid()`, `setpgid_execed_result()`,
    `memlock_prepare_range()`, `memlock_range_flag_result()`,
    `range_has_disallowed_change_flags()`, `munmap_prepare_range()`, and
    `mprotect_prepare_range()`.
  - memory-syscall and NUMA-policy validation helpers are Rust-owned:
    `mlockall_policy_result()`, `remap_file_pages_prepare()`,
    `mremap_prepare_args()`, `mremap_fixed_range_result()`,
    `mremap_maymove_result()`, `msync_prepare_range()`,
    `msync_locked_range_result()`, `mbind_prepare_range()`,
    `mempolicy_nodemask_bits_result()`,
    `mempolicy_nodemask_bits_is_clamped()`, `mbind_mode_flags_result()`,
    `mempolicy_mode_is_supported()`, `set_mempolicy_normalize_mode()`,
    `get_mempolicy_validate()`, and `move_pages_policy_result()`.
  - `brk()`, `mincore()`, and `do_mmap()` policy helpers are Rust-owned:
    `brk_prepare_result()`, `brk_default_vrflags()`,
    `mincore_prepare_range()`, `mmap_base_vrflags()`,
    `mmap_populated_mapping_result()`, `mmap_should_set_host_ro()`,
    `mmap_update_private_maxprot()`, `mmap_prot_denied_result()`,
    `mmap_maxprot_to_vrflags()`, `mmap_should_force_straight()`, and
    `mmap_is_shared()`.
  - signal/time syscall policy helpers are Rust-owned:
    `tgkill_target_result()`, `rt_sigprocmask_validate()`,
    `rt_sigprocmask_apply()`, `rt_sigpending_size_result()`,
    `signalfd4_sigsetsize_result()`, `signalfd4_flags_result()`,
    `getrusage_who_result()`, `itimer_which_result()`,
    `itimer_is_real()`, `itimer_should_start()`,
    `clock_gettime_dispatch()`, `gettimeofday_dispatch()`, and
    `nanosleep_validate_timespec()`.
  - signal-wait and alternate-signal-stack policy helpers are Rust-owned:
    `rt_sigtimedwait_prepare()`, `rt_sigtimedwait_timeout_result()`,
    `rt_sigtimedwait_prepare_masks()`, `rt_sigtimedwait_deadline()`,
    `rt_sigtimedwait_timeout_expired()`, `sigmask_to_signal_number()`,
    `rt_sigqueueinfo_pid_result()`, `sigsuspend_sigsetsize_result()`,

    `process_vm_validate_args()`, `process_vm_op_is_write()`,

    `ptrace_status_allows_io()`, `ptrace_setoptions_flags_result()`,
    `ptrace_apply_options()`, `ptrace_child_traced_result()`,
    `ptrace_attach_policy_result()`, `ptrace_detach_state_result()`, and
    `ptrace_siginfo_state_result()`.
  - wait4/waitid process/thread wait policy helpers are Rust-owned:
    `wait4_options_result()`, `waitid_to_wait_pid_result()`,
    `waitid_options_result()`, `wait_should_scan_process_result()`,
    `wait_should_scan_thread_result()`, `wait_process_pid_matches_result()`,
    `wait_thread_tid_matches_result()`,
    `wait_process_exited_candidate_result()`,
    `wait_thread_exited_candidate_result()`,
    `wait_nonptraced_stop_candidate_result()`,
    `wait_ptraced_stop_candidate_result()`,
    `wait_continued_candidate_result()`, `wait_reap_needed_result()`,
    `wait_nohang_result()`, `wait_empty_result()`,
    `wait_stopped_status_result()`, `wait_continued_status_result()`,
    `wait_zombie_skip_host_result()`, `wait_thread_empty_candidate_result()`,
    and `waitid_status_code_result()`.
  - clone, execveat, ptrace-event, and futex command policy helpers are
    Rust-owned: `clone_pthread_marker_result()`, `clone_flags_result()`,
    `clone_host_parent_flags_result()`, `clone_report_thread_result()`,
    `ptrace_exec_event_signal_result()`,
    `ptrace_syscall_event_signal_result()`, `ptrace_clone_event_result()`,

    `futex_requeue_val2_result()`, and `futex_timeout_ns_result()`.

Exit criteria:
- V3 through V5 pass.
- C fallback still builds.

Phase 4: Page allocator
-----------------------

Scope:
- Port allocator internals from lib/page_alloc.c.
- Preserve exported allocator symbols.
- Keep physical page accounting behavior identical.
- Add stress for allocate, free, split, merge, reserve, and exhaustion paths.
- Current completed slices:
  - rbtree free/allocate helper cluster is Rust-owned.
  - rbtree reserve/get-root helper pair is Rust-owned.
  - bitmap-backed no-lock helper cluster is Rust-owned while exported C
    wrappers still own MCS locking and IRQ state.
  - zero-free no-lock helper is Rust-owned while the exported C wrapper still
    owns locking and logging.
  - NUMA add-free helper body is Rust-owned while the exported C wrapper still
    owns error logging.
  - NUMA zero-free single-node helper is Rust-owned while the exported C
    wrapper still owns all-node traversal and the `zero_at_free` policy check.
  - NUMA allocation no-lock body is Rust-owned while the exported C wrapper
    still owns CPU-local-cache precheck, MCS lock/unlock, and debug logging.
  - NUMA direct free-to-tree helper is Rust-owned while the exported C wrapper
    still owns bounds checks, zero-now policy, locking, and logging.
  - NUMA deferred-free enqueue helper is Rust-owned while the exported C
    wrapper still owns public orchestration and IKC/Linux zero requests.
  - NUMA free-path preflight is Rust-owned while the exported C wrapper still
    owns CPU-local-cache handling, MCS lock/unlock, logging, and IKC/Linux
    zero requests.
- Next safe slices:
  - Widen V12 allocator stress around direct free, deferred free, zero-list
    movement, zero-at-free transitions, and accounting invariants.
  - Keep the IKC/Linux zero-request path and full exported
    `ihk_numa_free_pages()` lock/log orchestration in C until stress coverage
    covers deferred frees across CPUs and zero-at-free transitions.
- Build cadence:
  - Prefer equivalence, diff hygiene, shell syntax, Rust symbol, and SIMD
    checks after each small Rust helper batch.
  - Run full Rust/C image builds and V10/V11 runtime gates only after a larger
    conversion batch or a high-risk allocator behavior change.

Exit criteria:
- Allocator V12 stress passes.
- V4 through V11 pass on the runtime platform.

Phase 5: x86_64 memory core
---------------------------

Scope:
- Port PTE constants and helpers first.
- Port page-table walking.
- Port map, unmap, clear, and attribute-change paths.
- Port init page-table setup only after runtime mapping tests are stable.
- Leave assembly entry, interrupt, context-switch, and trampoline code as
  assembly.
- Current completed slice:
  - Syscall-side VM range validation helpers for `mlock()`, `munlock()`,
    `munmap()`, and `mprotect()` are Rust-owned while range-tree mutation,
    page-table changes, host VMA updates, and locking remain C-owned.
  - Additional memory-syscall validation helpers for `remap_file_pages()`,
    `mremap()`, `msync()`, `mbind()`, mempolicy, and `move_pages()` are
    Rust-owned while map/remap/sync execution, NUMA mask copy/verification,
    migration allocation, and range-tree mutation remain C-owned.
  - `brk()`, `mincore()`, and `do_mmap()` validation/policy helpers are
    Rust-owned while free-space search, object creation, page allocation,
    host VMA updates, range insertion, prefaulting, and teardown remain
    C-owned.
  - x86_64 page-attribute translation helpers for L3/L2/L1 attributes are
    Rust-owned while page-table walking, mapping, unmapping, TLB-sensitive
    mutation, and user-copy remain C-owned.
  - x86_64 smaller-page-size selection is Rust-owned while page-table
    splitting, TLB flushes, and mapping mutation remain C-owned.

Exit criteria:
- large_page/x86_64 tests pass.
- Mapping and attribute tests pass.
- V4 through V11 pass.

Phase 6: Process VM
-------------------

Scope:
- Port vm_range and process_vm operations.
- Port range-tree ownership and lookup logic.
- Port refcount helpers once layout and lifetime rules are tested.
- Keep external process lifecycle symbols stable.

Phase 7: Syscall core
---------------------

Scope:
- Port generic syscall dispatch.
- Port syscall families in this order:
  memory, process lifecycle, signal/wait, futex, file/object, remaining leaf
  handlers.
- Keep x86_64 syscall entry glue in C/assembly until generic handling is
  stable.
- Current completed slices:
  - SysV shared-memory ID, shmid construction, permission, and memory-lock
    rlimit helpers are Rust-owned while object lookup, locking, user-copy,
    mapping, and syscall orchestration remain C-owned.
  - Scheduler priority min/max, scheduler policy validation, RR interval,
    affinity permission, and affinity length helpers are Rust-owned while
    syscall wrappers, scheduler/thread lookup, mask mutation, migration
    requests, Linux permission forwarding, and user-copy remain C-owned.
  - prlimit resource validation, soft/hard ordering, Linux-forwarding
    resource detection, and McKernel resource mapping are Rust-owned while
    process lookup, locks, user-copy, storage, and Linux forwarding remain
    C-owned.
  - robust-list length checks, `tkill` tid validation, signal-action
    restrictions, `setpgid` normalization/execed checks, memory-lock range
    preparation, memory-range flag rejection, `munmap` range validation, and
    `mprotect` range validation are Rust-owned while signal state, process
    lookup, VM mutation, locking, and user-copy remain C-owned.
  - `mlockall`, `remap_file_pages`, `mremap`, `msync`, `mbind`,
    `set_mempolicy`, `get_mempolicy`, and `move_pages` validation/policy
    helpers are Rust-owned while the stateful syscall bodies remain C-owned.
  - `brk()`, `mincore()`, and `do_mmap()` policy helpers are Rust-owned while
    the stateful VM/mapping bodies remain C-owned.
  - signal/time syscall policy helpers for `tgkill`, `rt_sigprocmask`,
    `rt_sigpending`, `signalfd4`, `getrusage`, itimers, `clock_gettime`,
    `gettimeofday`, and `nanosleep` are Rust-owned while signal queues,
    user-copy, timer state mutation, CPU-time accounting walks, and Linux
    forwarding remain C-owned.
  - signal-wait and alternate-signal-stack policy helpers for
    `rt_sigtimedwait`, `rt_sigqueueinfo`, `rt_sigsuspend`, `rt_sigaction`, and
    `sigaltstack` are Rust-owned while pending-signal list traversal, signal
    delivery, action storage, thread state transitions, alternate-stack
    storage updates, and user-copy remain C-owned.
  - ptrace policy and process-vm wrapper validation helpers are Rust-owned while
    ptrace lookup/state mutation, register access, event delivery, cross-process
    VM copying, and user-copy remain C-owned.

Exit criteria:
- syscall delegation tests pass.
- strace-focused tests pass.
- V4 through V11 pass.

Phase 8: Scheduler, wait, timer, and futex
------------------------------------------

Scope:
- Port scheduling policy logic.
- Port wait queues and wakeups.
- Port timer coordination.
- Port futex logic after syscall and process dependencies are stable.
- Keep low-level context-switch assembly.
- Current completed slices:
  - waitqueue locked list core helpers are Rust-owned while scheduler callback
    execution and locking wrappers remain C-owned.
  - scheduler priority, policy, RR interval, and affinity helpers are
    Rust-owned while scheduler state mutation remains C-owned.
  - signal-mask policy, itimer selector/start decisions, clock dispatch
    decisions, and nanosleep timespec validation are Rust-owned while signal
    queues, actual timer state mutation, CPU-time accounting, and forwarding
    remain C-owned.
  - signal-wait mask/deadline/expiry policy and alternate-stack validation are
    Rust-owned while pending-list traversal, signal delivery, and thread state
    transitions remain C-owned.

Exit criteria:
- Futex tests pass repeatedly.
- Scheduler stress passes.
- V4 through V11 pass.

Phase 9: Kernel objects and filesystems
---------------------------------------

Scope:
- Port file objects, shared memory objects, zero objects, dev objects, hugefile
  objects, pager, freeze, procfs, sysfs, gencore, and xpmem.
- Preserve procfs/sysfs text formats unless a separate compatibility-breaking
  plan approves changes.
- Current completed slices:
  - XPMEM make/get/access policy helpers are Rust-owned:
    `xpmem_make_initial_policy_result()`, `xpmem_make_alignment_result()`,
    `xpmem_get_policy_result()`, `xpmem_perms_result()`,
    `xpmem_check_permit_mode_result()`, `xpmem_validate_access_result()`, and
    `xpmem_attach_overlap_result()`. XPMEM object lifetime, references,
    attach/remap/page-table operations, and user-copy remain C-owned.
  - XPMEM ID/hash/owner/attach/detach/destroying-state policy helpers are
    Rust-owned: `xpmem_id_to_tgid_result()`,
    `xpmem_tg_hashtable_index_result()`,
    `xpmem_ap_hashtable_index_result()`, `xpmem_make_id_result()`,
    `xpmem_positive_id_result()`, `xpmem_owner_policy_result()`,
    `xpmem_attach_initial_policy_result()`,
    `xpmem_destroying_state_result()`, `xpmem_is_destroying_result()`,
    `xpmem_destroying_error_result()`,
    `xpmem_two_destroying_error_result()`,
    `xpmem_three_destroying_error_result()`,
    `xpmem_attach_destroying_result()`, and
    `xpmem_detach_lookup_result()`. XPMEM object allocation, reference
    mutation, lock/list mutation, remap/page-table operations, and user-copy
    remain C-owned.
  - XPMEM range/page-table decision helpers are Rust-owned:
    `xpmem_remove_range_step_result()`,
    `xpmem_remove_memory_range_action_result()`,
    `xpmem_range_private_invalid_result()`,
    `xpmem_clear_pte_range_result()`, `xpmem_fault_vaddr_result()`,
    `xpmem_straight_phys_result()`, `xpmem_remote_pte_missing_result()`,
    `xpmem_seg_phys_plus_off_result()`, `xpmem_att_page_fits_result()`,
    `xpmem_pte_mismatch_result()`, and `xpmem_unpin_step_result()`. Range
    splitting/freeing, attachment list mutation, PTE installation, pin/unpin
    accounting mutation, and user-copy remain C-owned.
  - XPMEM refcount/object-list and destroy-state decision helpers are
    Rust-owned: `xpmem_close_decision_result()`,
    `xpmem_ref_drop_should_free_result()`,
    `xpmem_begin_destroy_result()`, `xpmem_finish_destroy_result()`, and
    `xpmem_object_lookup_decision_result()`. Atomic reference mutation, frees,
    lock/list mutation, attach/remap/page-table mutation, and user-copy remain
    C-owned.
  - File/dev/procfs/sysfs/pager/shmobj/hugefile/shared-memobj object decision
    helpers are Rust-owned through eight bounded C ABI batches, including
    file-object page/page-I/O/allocation decisions, device-object PFN and map-size
    decisions, sysfs path/packet/parameter/wait/handler/allocation/callback
    decisions, procfs mem/pagemap/status/maps/auxv/task/backlog/cmdline/pbuf
    decisions, pager arealist/mlock/pagein/pageout policy math, SysV
    shm/shmlock lifecycle and page-state decisions, and hugefile
    presence/growth decisions, plus shared memobj op/default/flush/free/remap
    predicates, page mode/refcount classifiers, procfs entry/comm
    classification, pager Linux I/O loop policy, pager copy-fault policy, file
    sref math, and file premap interleave policy. Allocation, reference
    mutation, locks, lists, page-table mutation, page I/O, pager orchestration,
    sysfs IKC exchange, procfs buffer mutation, and user-copy remain C-owned.
- Next safe slices:
  - Keep the direct `/dev/xpmem` runtime gate active.
  - Expand file/dev/procfs/sysfs runtime coverage around file-backed mmap,
    `/proc/PID/pagemap`, `/proc/PID/mem`, sysfs create/show/store, device PFN
    mappings, and hugetlbfs-backed mappings before moving page-cache, procfs
    buffer, sysfs IKC, device-PFN, or hugefile allocation/mutation bodies.
  - Add direct pager pagein/pageout/swapout workload probes before moving pager
    orchestration, Linux forwarding, user-copy, allocation/free, or page-table
    mutation out of C.
  - Keep SysV shm runtime smoke in the gate for future shmobj changes; add
    focused LTP shmat/shmget/shmdt coverage before moving object allocation,
    refcount mutation, or page-list mutation.
  - Continue with additional XPMEM lifecycle decision helpers only where the
    focused XPMEM suite can exercise the path; keep attach/remap mutation,
    lock/list mutation, page-table mutation, and frees in C until broader
    runtime coverage exists.

Exit criteria:
- mcstat passes.
- procfs/sysfs reads pass.
- xpmem tests pass.
- dump and object lifecycle tests pass.
- V4 through V11 pass.

Phase 11: Host Linux modules
----------------------------

Scope:
- Port mcctrl logic first where feasible.
- For ihk and ihk-smp, use Rust external-module support only on kernels that
  support it.
- Where external Rust modules are not practical, keep minimal C module shims and
  move pure policy logic into Rust behind C ABI.
- Current Rust-owned foothold: Kbuild can accept prebuilt Rust objects; `ihk`
  links a no_std Rust helper object for page-allocator bitmap math and
  open-path validation predicates; `ihk-smp-x86_64` links a no_std Rust helper
  object for dump bitmap math and OS-status gate predicates; and `mcctrl`
  links a no_std Rust helper object for binfmt/path whitelist decisions, pager
  path/fs/device decisions, procfs mcos/pid/tid name formatting, and futex
  command/private/realtime decode plus labels. Refcount mutation, lock/list
  mutation, device allocation, memory registration, IKC exchange, and
  module-load runtime coverage remain pending.

Exit criteria:
- Rocky VM builds ihk_ko, ihk-smp-x86_64_ko, and mcctrl_ko.
- Modules load.
- McKernel boots.
- V7 through V11 pass.

Phase 12: User tools
--------------------

Scope:
- Port mcexec, mcstat, crash helpers, dump helpers, and smaller utilities.
- Preserve command-line flags, output formats, exit codes, and protocol
  behavior.
- Current Rust-owned foothold: `mcstat` links a Rust helper object for memory
  scale/unit selection, periodic header cadence, monitor-status labels, and
  OS-status labels;
  `mcexec` links a Rust helper object for option integer/flag/mcos-id parsing,
  extra-env list count/search/allocation/copy/free, byte-size parsing, stack
  premap/max parsing, saved stack-limit env parsing/application,
  heap-extension defaulting, thread-count selection, memory-policy flags, OMPI
  memory-policy classification, FLIB affinity CPU-list shifting, LD_PRELOAD
  string construction, overlay fd path preparation, execveat path construction,
  stack-limit adjustment, getdents dirent field accessors, and proc-task leaf
  path classification; `ihklib` links a Rust helper object for
  CPU-list parsing, memory-size parsing, memory-list parsing, IKC-map parsing,
  cpuset.mems parsing/allocation, request-to-string formatting, meminfo path
  formatting, packed-env parsing, create-os env parsing, and first-match env
  lookup, reserve-memory config parsing, environment action classification,
  parsed-env cleanup, and device path formatting for simple `*_str` wrappers;
  `mcinspect` links a Rust helper object for status labels, pid/vtop parsing,
  action validation, and mcos path formatting; `eclair` links a Rust helper
  object for physmem section-name formatting, mcos path formatting, and GDB
  remote hex/binary packet formatting; the crash extension links a Rust helper
  object for x86 address-translation decisions, symbol/add-symbol command
  formatting, thread-status labels, VM permission/default-backing labels,
  rbtree position formatting, page-size labels, and kmsg ring split math;
  `ihkconfig` and `ihkosctl` route generic integer parsing, hex parsing,
  OS-status labels, and mcd/mcos path formatting through Rust when Rust user
  helpers are enabled. Device I/O, ioctl handling, output loops, DWARF/BFD
  traversal, crash command orchestration, and most IHK command mutation remain
  C-owned.

Exit criteria:
- Existing scripts work unchanged.
- Tool output diffs are empty or explicitly approved.

Phase 13: arm64
---------------

Scope:
- Bring arm64 onto the stabilized Rust abstractions after x86_64 stops
  changing rapidly.
- Keep arm64 assembly where required.
- Add architecture-specific ABI/layout checks.

Exit criteria:
- arm64 build passes.
- arm64 smoke tests pass on suitable hardware or emulator support.

Phase 14: Final C retirement
----------------------------

Scope:
- Remove replaced C implementations after Rust equivalence and runtime
  validation.
- Keep compatibility headers where external callers depend on them.
- Remove C fallback only after a separate compatibility-breaking approval.
- Audit remaining unsafe Rust blocks and C shims.

Exit criteria:
- McKernel-owned non-assembly implementation logic is Rust-owned.
- Remaining C is limited to compatibility shims, unavoidable module glue,
  generated bindings, or assembly-adjacent code.

Operating rules for every phase
------------------------------------------------------------

- One subsystem moves at a time.
- Each phase must add or update its own narrow verification.
- C and Rust behavior must be proven equivalent before removing C code.
- Runtime smoke testing must happen before broad regression testing.
- If a verification step fails, stop at that step and log the exact failure in
  kernel.log.
- Keep the system bootable after every merge.

Progress update 2026-05-20 17:29 EDT
------------------------------------------------------------

- Added the first validated IHK Linux-module Rust build/link path on Rocky
  x86_64 without making Rust mandatory for fallback builds.
- Extended the shared Kbuild CMake wrapper with `PREBUILT_OBJECTS` so a module
  can link generated Rust `.o` files alongside C objects.
- Added a Kbuild `.cmd` sidecar writer for prebuilt Rust objects, preventing
  noisy Kbuild missing-command-file warnings.
- Added `ihk/linux/core/rust/core_helpers.rs` and linked it into `ihk.ko` when
  `ENABLE_RUST_IHK_MODULE_HELPERS` is on. Rust now owns IHK core
  page-allocator bitmap index/bit/alignment math and OS/device open-path
  validity/shareability predicates; C still owns refcount mutation, allocation,
  device arrays, locks, and callbacks.
- Added `ihk/linux/driver/smp/rust/smp_driver_helpers.rs` and linked it into
  `ihk-smp-x86_64.ko` when `ENABLE_RUST_IHK_MODULE_HELPERS` is on. Rust now
  owns dump bitmap index/bit/count/end math and OS-status initial predicates;
  C still owns boot sequencing, list walks, page allocation, status mutation,
  and wakeup/IKC paths.
- Verified Rust-enabled module builds and C-fallback module builds. `mcctrl`
  still builds through the existing C Kbuild path; Rust linkage for mcctrl is
  the next module milestone.
- Honest estimates after this slice: McKernel core helper/decision surface
  remains 99%, whole-tree Rust ownership with arm64 deferred is 56%, IHK module
  ownership is 8%, and user-tool ownership remains 49%. This did not reach the
  requested 59%/100% target because the new module slices are intentionally
  small and runtime-safe.

- Honest estimates after this slice: McKernel core helper/decision surface
  remains 99%, whole-tree Rust ownership with arm64 deferred is 59%, IHK module
  ownership is 20%, and user-tool ownership remains 49%. This did not reach the
  requested 65%/100% target because mutation-heavy kernel/module bodies and
  module-load runtime validation remain pending.
- Path forward:
  - The next biggest honest jump should be either module-load smoke followed by
    Rust ownership of a small mutation body, or a larger user-tool conversion
    where runtime validation is cheaper.
  - Keep the helper bridge until Rust can own complete module bodies; then
    collapse the C call sites into thin ABI shims or remove them.

- Continued the whole-tree Rust conversion with two validated user-tool
