  - Added Rust-owned mcinspect helpers for DWARF byte-size/member-offset form
    classification, signed-size/offset validation, plus-uconst expression
    validation, global-address form classification, address-expression
    validation, structure/member/global tag-name matching, thread active
    marker selection, command basename selection, CPU-local address math,
    sched-list-to-thread address derivation, and vtop process-selection
    decisions.
  - Routed `dwarf_get_size()`, `dwarf_get_offset()`,
    `dwarf_struct_field_offset()`, `dwarf_get_address()`,
    `dwarf_global_var_addr()`, `print_thread()`, `mcps()`, `find_proc()`, and
    `mcvtop()` through those Rust helpers with the C fallback preserved.
  - Added Rust-owned mcstat helpers for CLI delay/count parsing, `/dev/mcosN`
    path construction, NUMA memory total aggregation, current memory usage
    aggregation, max memory usage aggregation, and OS-status labels.
  - Corrected the existing Rust mcstat monitor-status constants so the
    freeze/frozen/thaw/panic values match `ihk/linux/include/ihk/ihk_monitor.h`.
  - Kept device I/O, ioctl handling, DWARF/BFD traversal, kernel memory reads,
    output emission, and long-running command orchestration in C.
- Why helpers and C edits are still present:
  - The helper boundary is a temporary staged bridge. Rust owns pure logic
    behind stable C ABI calls first; the C entrypoints keep side effects and
    runtime coverage intact until the Rust path can replace whole bodies
    safely.
  - Editing C call sites is necessary during the bridge phase because the
    existing binaries still enter through C functions. Each C edit routes a
    validated decision slice into Rust while keeping a C fallback buildable.
- Local validation:
  - `rustfmt` passed for `executer/user/rust/mcinspect_helpers.rs` and
    `tools/mcstat/rust/mcstat_helpers.rs`.
  - Standalone `rustc` object checks passed for both helper files.
  - `nm -u` on both standalone helper objects reported no unresolved symbols.
  - Rust-enabled focused builds passed for `mcinspect` and `mcstat`.
  - C-fallback focused builds passed for `mcinspect` and `mcstat`.
  - Rust-enabled help smoke passed for `/tmp/mckernel-rocky-rust/tools/mcstat/mcstat -h`.
  - Rust-enabled help smoke passed for `/tmp/mckernel-rocky-rust/executer/user/mcinspect --help`.
  - C-fallback help smoke passed for both tools.
  - Rust-enabled broad target build passed for `ihkconfig`, `ihkosctl`,
    `ihkmond`, `mcstat`, `mcexec`, `mcinspect`, `eclair`, `mckernel.img`,
    `ihk_ko`, `ihk-smp-x86_64_ko`, and `mcctrl_ko`.

    edits.

    before ledger edits.

  - McKernel core helper/decision-surface Rust ownership remains 99%.

Boot-path verification safety plan:
- Do not run host `mcreboot.sh`, host reboot/shutdown/kexec, or scripts that
  implicitly call them without explicit approval for that specific run.
- Use `scripts/qemu-mckernel-guest.sh` as the safe guest boundary for future
  boot-path verification. It creates a disposable qcow2 overlay, captures a
  serial log under `/tmp`, runs requested commands only inside the guest over
  SSH, refuses host device image paths, and attempts guest
  `mcstop+release.sh -k` cleanup when SSH became ready.
- All `mcreboot.sh`, `mcstop+release.sh`, module load/unload, and V9/V10/V11
  boot smoke commands must run inside that guest unless the user explicitly
  approves a host/VM script for that exact run.
- Required remaining guardrails for real boot attempts: use a Rocky 8.x guest
  image or disposable generated equivalent, timeout every boot/test command,
  capture guest dmesg/kmsg artifacts to `/tmp`, never pass host `/dev/mcos*`
  or host IHK modules into the guest, and destroy/revert the overlay after each
  attempt.
- Safe non-reboot boot-adjacent checks remain image builds,
  `scripts/ihk-module-load-smoke.sh` when host module load is approved, and
  `scripts/rocky-v11-focused.sh --classify-only`, `--build-only`, or
  `--skip-boot`. Plain `scripts/rocky-v11-focused.sh` is not safe by default
  because it can call `mcreboot.sh`.

    zeroing, lock-pointer publication, and null-argument no-op behavior.

    wait-q digest mixed a stack pointer value that differs between the C and
